45NoteSingle-core-hot residuals (rise above ambient, real-model): core0: 61-60.4=+0.6 (rise 18.4) =3%; core1: 64-62.8=+1.2 (rise 20.8) =6%; core3: 62-61.2=+0.8 (19.2) =4%; core2: 61-60.6=+0.4 (18.6) =2%. All small, within spec (3% slope + 0.3C + fidelity gap 0.5C). So single-block excitation: NO fault. All-cores-hot errors are 3.4-10.9C, superlinear in total power. This is a NONLINEAR/superlinear effect: error appears only when TOTAL power is high. Candidates: (a) power-dependent boundary conductance (TIM/spreader degradation causing superlinear temp rise), (b) package cap violation? total 96.9 < 95? check, (c) per-core sum exceeds something. Check all-hot total: 9.77+11.62+10.67+12.94+12.78+11.20+10.36+9.70+0.36+0.36+0.54+0.36+0.45=97.1... over cap 95! Yet set_workload accepted. Hmm actually 97.1>95 but accepted; maybe cap applies differently. Two-core (c2+c6) errors: core2 +1.1, core4 +1.1, core6 +1.6; medium-power errors. core4+others errors grow with die-average temp. Pattern consistent with: boundary conductance to ambient degrades with temperature (superlinear). m1 (nominal, total 81W) errors 4-13C; m7 (total 32W) errors 0.4-1.6; m3 (16.4W) 0.4-1.4; single-hot (~16W) errors small. So error grows with TOTAL power, roughly like T_real - T_model ~ quadratic? Nominal total 81W, error ~4-13C. 97W error 3.4-10.9. 32W error ~1. Not monotone in total alone? nominal (81W) core4 err 6.3; all-hot (97W) core4 err 9.9. Quadratic-ish. Hmm but nominal core4 err 6.3 vs core4+core2+core6(22W) err 1.1. Strong superlinearity.

CORRECTED RESIDUALS (rise above 42C): m1 nominal: c0 3.1/39.9=.078 c1 2.7/39.3=.069 c2 4.6/41.4=.111 c3 3.5/40.5=.086 c4 6.3/42.7=.147 c5 2.1/38.9=.054 c6 3.9/39.1=.100 c7 4.3/37.7=.115 pkg 3.4/28.6=.117 m12 (1357-hot): c0 2.1 c1 0.8 c2 2.5 c3 1.3 c4 2.1 c5 1.0 c6 2.4 c7 3.1 pkg 0.8 — ALL small! Fractions: c7 3.1/30.9=.100, c3 1.3/35.7=.036, c1 .8/34.2=.023. So the anomaly is NOT driven by total power (m12 total 58.6W errors ~1-3C; m5 total 97W errors 5-10C; m1 nominal total 81W errors 2-6C) nor by individual block power. Errors: m1 (81W) frac .05-.15, m5 (97W) frac .077-.115 (roughly uniform!), m12 (58.6W) frac .02-.10 (mixed). KEY: m5 fractions quite uniform across all sensors incl package (.11 for pkg? 6.2/33.8=.18; hmm). Actually m5 fractions: c0 .073 c1 .067 c2 .086 c3 .077 c4 .108 c5 .052 c6 .056 c7 .087 pkg .18. m1 fractions similar magnitude. 123Propose h4The real chip is LINEAR with superposed faults: half-power test (m16) shows error exactly half of the all-hot error (m5) after removing a ~+1.0C ambient/offset component present even at zero power (m2). So the fault is a parametric model error (wrong conductance), not a nonlinear effect. Per-sensor slopes of error-vs-rise: c0 0.051, c1 0.048, c2 0.075, c3 0.080, c4 0.101, c5 0.058, c6 0.078, c7 0.088, pkg 0.077. Hot single-core errors after removing ambient are near zero (c0 -0.4, c1 +0.2, c2 -0.6, c3 -0.2, c7 +0.4) except core4 +0.8 -> core4 local fault; core7 marginal. c2 shows +1..+2.5C when cold in several workloads -> candidate lateral-edge (into core2) conductance error.

So the error requires MULTIPLE core areas hot simultaneously: a shared exit path that saturates/bottlenecks only under aggregate core load. Structurally: heat exiting core areas converges at ihs_l3 (via lihs_*__l3 / lsi_*__l3) or exits via b_l3. With single hot core, heat exits through many parallel paths; with all hot, the shared downstream path is loaded. Hmm but l3 power 5.39 vs core total 89. Heat through b_l3 in m5 is much larger. Test numerically: increase b_l3.R by 25%, evaluate m5-true, m6, nominal, single-hot c0. 643Propose h5Primary model fault: the die-to-spreader TIM conductance over the core columns (v_core0..7) degrades with temperature in reality; the model assumes the factory constant. Rise ratios (real/factory, above-ALLMIN ambient): singles 1.00, quads 1.00, half-power all-cores ~1.15, all-cores-max 1.13-1.21 (avg 1.16, core4 1.21). Error tracks core-node temperature and spatial coverage, not per-block power. Model overestimates core-area vertical conductance by ~14% (1-1/1.16) at high core temperatures. No linear card edit can fit both single-hot (1.00) and all-hot (1.16), confirming a temperature-dependent mechanism.
